here.** Emotion recognition in schools banned since Feb 2025; schools as *deployers* not
providers; Annex III education duties at **2 Dec 2027** against a blog's **2 Aug 2026**; a claimed
*Regulation (EU) 2026/1744* Digital Omnibus. 🔴 **`eur-lex.europa.eu` and
`artificialintelligenceact.eu` answer `000` on `curl` and `ENOTFOUND` on `WebFetch`.** 🔵 **An
EMEA engagement's conformity dates must be taken from the Official Journal, not from this page.**


#### 🆕 🟢 p141 — EMEA gains four rows on measured evidence, and the cleanest Maven artefact on the shelf is Oxford's

🟢 **🆕 A Maven `groupId` is origin evidence, and it is stronger than an npm author field.**
`uk.ac.ox.ctl:spring-security-lti13` **0.3.7** — LTI 1.3 for Spring Security — places 🟢 **EMEA**
with no inference: `uk.ac.ox` is the University of Oxford's reverse-DNS namespace, and
publishing into it requires controlling it, where filling in an author field does not. 🟢 **It is
also the cleanest Maven row on these shelves** — Apache-2.0 declared in its own POM at depth 1,
against a population where **5 of 9 declare nothing**
([`p141-pom-chain-sweep`](../compose/code/p141-pom-chain-sweep/README.md)). 🔴 **It ships no
licence text in its 72-entry jar**, so `T21`'s Maven figure rises to **83%**.

🆕 🟢 **Three further EMEA rows placed on commit offsets**
([`p141-repo-origin-layer`](../compose/code/p141-repo-origin-layer/README.md)):

| row | evidence | note |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 `ahmedEid1/lumen` | `+0200` on **828 of 828** commits | 🔴 GPL-3.0 — course-scoped RAG + MCP, **10 tools, all dynamically registered** |
| 🟢 `hesham0-0nasser/tutor-lms-mcp` | `+0300` on 7 of 7 | 🆕 **WordPress + Tutor LMS**, 31 tools, 🔴 17 writes, 🔴 **no licence file** |
| 🟢 `ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp` | `+0200` on 76 commits | 🟢 MIT; the only row on these shelves with a **database-enforced immutable pedagogical audit log** |

🔵 **`tutor-mcp` is the EMEA row that matters for a regulated engagement**, and `ACTION CX`
established why: its `pedagogical_decisions` table is append-only by database trigger on both
SQLite and Postgres, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`-isolated per tenant, and bound one-to-one to the
assessment it produced — with a trigger refusing the insert unless the attempt's concept matches
the decision snapshot's own `target_concept`. 🟢 **That is EU AI Act Article 14 / Annex III
auditability implemented in the engine**, by one MIT maintainer, ahead of any vendor on this
page.

🔴 **The EMEA search channel is the thinnest of the four**: the only education-specific item
eight queries returned was a Council of Europe working conference from **October 2024**. 🔵
**That is a gap in the channel, not in the region** — the artefact reads above placed four EMEA
rows in the same hour the demand channel placed none.


#### 🆕 p140 — EMEA is the first region this KB has placed on MEASURED evidence, and it took nine passes

🟢 **🆕 `ACTION CU` booked EMEA on a hostname compiled into shipped code.**
[`eth-moodle-mcp`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/eth-moodle-mcp) **1.3.3** carries
`https://moodle-app2.let.ethz.ch` **four times** and `https://safari.ethz.ch` once — **ETH
Zürich's** production Moodle (run by its *Lehrentwicklung und -technologie* unit) and its SAFARI
course platform.

🔵 **Why this is different in kind from every previous regional attribution on this page.** p139
called this row *"the first EMEA-owned connector measured at the tool layer"* on the strength of
its `ddca_*` tool descriptions — which is a reading of prose. 🟢 **A compiled-in hostname is a
binding**: the server does not function pointed anywhere else, and an author field can claim any
country while a request path cannot. 🔴 **Nine passes of ownership gap were a failure of looking
at the layer where looking works** (`T29`).

🔴 **The region's correction this pass is about Sakai, and it raises the review burden.** p139 told
this page that Sakai's two kernel artefacts had *"opposite licence posture"*. 🔴 **Both are
ECL-2.0** — `sakai-kernel-impl` 23.3 inherits it from `org.sakaiproject:oss-parent:1`, three levels
up. 🔵 **For a European university running Sakai, the practical consequence is worse than the
original claim**: the grant is **ECL-2.0**, *education's own licence*, which off-the-shelf scanners
do not recognise; it is declared in a sibling POM three levels up; and **neither jar ships any
licence text**. 🟢 **The fix is build configuration, not law** — `org.imsglobal:basiclti-util`
1.2.0 ships `META-INF/LICENSE` and `META-INF/NOTICE` correctly — which is the sentence to put in
front of a platform's maintainers.

🔴 **No new EMEA instrument.** The channel returned the EU AI Act omnibus dates (already `T16`),
the ~$2.64B regional figure, the OECD *Digital Education Outlook*, the Finland/Estonia/Netherlands
grouping and the UK's £4M — 🔵 **all already carried**, and each was grepped against `intel/`
before being discarded, which is the `T24` rule working as designed. 🔴 **Still no primary source
read**: `eur-lex.europa.eu` and `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` answer `403` at `CONNECT`, and
this pass established the same refusal applies to `ethz.ch` — so it is an allowlist, not a
government-host exclusion.

🟡 **The EMEA coverage holes p139 recorded are unchanged**: this page still carries four **Jisc**
procurement routes the channel has never surfaced, and still has no EMEA ministry-side edtech
provider map.

#### 🆕 p139 — a funded skills instrument, and the first EMEA-owned connector measured at the tool layer

🟢 **🆕 The `AI Skills Academy` is operational since 2026-05-01**, funded under the **Digital Europe
Programme**, offering AI training including generative AI. 🔵 **This is the first EMEA instrument on
this page that is a *funded delivery vehicle* rather than a duty** — i.e. a route by which a studio's
curriculum or tooling work can be paid for, which is what p137's Jisc procurement finding was for
the UK.

🟡 **🆕 The Council of Europe's education ministers argue the private sector should be treated as a
key actor in regulating AI in education.** 🔵 **Worth one line because it is the opposite of the
posture most EU-level education texts take**, and it opens a standards-participation route for a
vendor rather than only a compliance obligation. 🔴 Non-binding; a conference position.

🔴 **Confirmed, not new — and `T24` records that this pass nearly double-published it.** The
**Digital Omnibus / Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** deferral of Annex III (education) from
**2026-08-02** to **2027-12-02**, with **Article 50 transparency undeferred from 2026-08-02**, is
already on this page and in `intel/trends.md` → `T16` (p137). 🟢 **The only increments this pass
found are two procedural dates — Council approval 2026-06-29, OJ publication 2026-07-24 — and they
are secondary.**

🔴 **The first EMEA-owned education connector measured at the tool layer, and it disagrees with
itself by 15 tools.** [`eth-moodle-mcp`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/eth-moodle-mcp) **1.3.3**
documents **3** tools under a heading that reads **"Works with Any Moodle"** and registers **18** —
including **ten `ddca_*` tools** that scrape one specific **ETH Zürich** course website, and
🔴 **`submit_assignment`, an undocumented write.** 🔵 **For an EMEA engagement this is the concrete
form of `T22`: the connector a Swiss or German institution would pick up off npm is substantially a
single-course scraper, and its write surface is undocumented.** Measured in
[`p139-tool-count-drift`](../compose/code/p139-tool-count-drift/README.md).

🟡 **One EMEA interoperability gap, measured rather than assumed**: `uk.ac.ox.ctl:spring-lti13`
(Oxford's LTI 1.3 Spring library, which this KB has cited as an attachment option) **is not on Maven
Central at all — `maven-metadata.xml` answers 404**. 🔵 **An institution cannot consume it through
the channel its build tool uses**, which makes it a source dependency rather than a library.


#### 🆕 p138 — EMEA is not the EU, and this page has been reading one of its three parts

🔴 **A structural gap in this KB's own region vocabulary, found by querying the region as a region.**
Every EMEA entry on this page for the last several passes has been **EU, UK or Nordic**: the AI Act
clocks, Jisc procurement, SURF, the OECD outlook. 🔵 **The Middle East and Africa are the other
two-thirds of the label, and they have had no rows at all.** First entries, named so later passes can
deepen them:

| jurisdiction | instrument | class |
|---|---|---|
| 🆕 **UAE** | **National AI Strategy 2031** | national strategy, not education-specific |
| 🆕 **Saudi Arabia** | **SDAIA** AI governance framework | national regulator with a published framework |
| 🆕 **Kenya, South Africa, Nigeria** | AI policies reported **in drafting** | 🔴 pre-instrument — nothing binding to comply with yet |

🔴 **Both the Gulf rows are economy-wide strategies, not education instruments**, and the three
African rows are drafts. 🔵 **So the measured finding is not "MEA regulates education AI" — it is
that MEA has national AI governance machinery and no education-specific duty**, which is the inverse
of the EU's position and a genuinely different engagement shape: there is no conformity deadline to
sell against, and the buyer is a ministry building capability rather than a school proving
compliance. 🔴 **Every one of these five rows is from a single secondary source whose subject was
insurance, not education.** They are recorded as leads, not as findings, and none should be quoted to
a client without a primary read.

🟡 **The EU picture is unchanged from p137 and still the one with dates**: Annex III education duties
deferred to **2027-12-02** by Regulation **(EU) 2026/1744**, with **Article 50 transparency applying
since 2026-08-02**. 🔵 That split — *the high-risk clock moved, the transparency obligation did not*
— remains this region's whole commercial argument, and `P15` is the pattern built on it.

🟡 **Adoption, weakly sourced and labelled**: the European AI-in-education market is put at
**USD 2.64B** in 2026, with **Finland, Estonia and the Netherlands** named as K-12 integration
leaders, and the **OECD's 2026 Digital Education Outlook** reported to favour purpose-built
educational AI over general-purpose tools. 🔴 **One secondary roundup for the first two; the OECD
report itself was not fetched this pass.**

#### 🆕 p137 — the education high-risk clock moved to 2027-12-02, and the obligation that survived is the one this KB already has instruments for

🔴 **The Digital Omnibus on AI is law, and it defers exactly the duties that education sits under.**
Parliament **2026-06-16**, Council **2026-06-29**, signed **2026-07-08**, published as **Regulation
(EU) 2026/1744**, in force **2026-07-27**:

| obligation | was | 🔴 now |
|---|---|---|
| **Annex III** stand-alone high-risk — *education is an Annex III use case* (admission, evaluation, exam scoring, proctoring) | 2026-08-02 | **2027-12-02** |
| **Annex I** high-risk embedded in regulated products | 2027-08-02 | **2028-08-02** |
| 🟢 **Article 50 transparency** (synthetic-content marking, AI-interaction disclosure) | 2026-08-02 | 🟢 **UNCHANGED — applied 2026-08-02**, with a grace period to **2026-12-02** for systems already on the market 🔴 *(one source says 2027-02-02; recorded as a conflict, not a date)* |

🟢 **So the sales motion inverts.** The conformity-assessment work a client was told to finish by
August 2026 now has **fourteen more months** — while the Article 50 marking and disclosure work is
**already late**. 🔵 **This KB is unusually well placed on exactly that half**: four committed
instruments (`compose/code/aiact-50-2-marking`, `-pack`, `-spans`, `-exposure`) implement the
Article 50(2) marking surface, and `compose/patterns.md` → **`P15`** turns them into an engagement.

🔴 **The deferral is a reprieve, not a repeal**, and the stated reason — national authorities and
harmonised technical standards were not ready — is itself a selling point: the standards a client
will be audited against do not exist yet, so the control set is still negotiable.

🟢 **And procurement is no longer absent.** `ACTION CC` found four Jisc routes (see
`verticals/solutions.md`), the sharpest being the **digital assessment tools framework**, whose
contract runs **2026-11-01 → 2029-10-31** — a three-year vehicle that an AI assessment layer can be
called off against without a new tender. 🔴 **SURF returned nothing**: the Dutch route is still
unmeasured rather than absent.

- **The regulatory clock moved, and the deferral is now confirmed as law.** High-risk obligations
  for Annex III education use cases (admissions, grading, proctoring) were **postponed to 2 December
  2027** under the Digital Omnibus on AI. 🟢 **p131 correction:** p130 recorded the Council as not
  yet having adopted and told the reader to treat the delay as "politically agreed, not final". The
  **Council's final green light is reported for 29 June 2026**, and this KB's own pages have carried
  the instrument as **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 of 8 July 2026** for several passes. **Plan against
  2 December 2027 as a legal date** (Annex I embedded systems: 2 August 2028).
- **What already binds, regardless of the delay:** emotion recognition in education has been
  **prohibited since 2 February 2025**; **AI literacy duties (Article 4) have applied since
  February 2025**; public schools face a fundamental-rights impact assessment.
- **Adoption is deliberately slow:** no generative AI tool has been approved for system-wide use in
  the European Schools network, and the approval procedure is still being written.
- **Opportunity:** the delay to December 2027 is a *sales window, not a reprieve* — institutions
  that start in 2026 can build conformity in rather than retrofit it. Concretely: AI inventory and
  classification against Annex III, vendor-contract review, AI-literacy programmes that discharge
  an obligation already in force, and architecture that keeps grading and admissions decisions
  human-made by construction. **Avoid emotion recognition entirely** — it is already illegal here,
  and products built elsewhere ship it.
- 🆕 **Two concrete EMEA openings p130 missed.** (1) **Proctoring is the asked-for capability the
  shelf cannot supply at grade** — four MIT projects exist, the largest is 18★, and three do
  affect-adjacent inference, which in an EU education setting is **prohibited outright** (Art. 5),
  not merely high-risk. An auditable, affect-free integrity system built for Annex III from the
  start has no open-source incumbent and a dated legal driver. (2) **The benchmark layer is
  EMEA-origin** — `eth-lre/mathtutorbench` (ETH Zurich, EMNLP 2025 oral) is the strongest
  evaluation asset in this KB, and its licence is unresolved; an EMEA engagement is the natural
  place to fund or negotiate that resolution.
- 🆕 **The UK has become a distinct market with its own delivery shape (p132).** Its first **AI
  Adoption Summit (8 June)** committed **£200m+** to AI adoption — two orders of magnitude above the
  £4M lesson-planning programme this KB already held. 🔴 **And the delivery structure is the
  finding, not the money:** government funds, **Big Tech delivers** (Cisco, IBM, BT, Rolls-Royce),
  trade unions legitimise, **Skills England** sets the curriculum framework — and **traditional
  education providers barely appear.** 🟢 **For a services firm that is the single most actionable
  structural fact on this page: the UK is routing education-AI money through systems integrators
  and vendors, not through EdTech companies.** That is Globant's shape of work, and the entry point
  is delivery partnership rather than product. Track the **QS/HolonIQ 2026 Europe EdTech 200** as
  the regional cohort list (the EMEA counterpart to the LATAM EdTech 100 held below).
- 🆕 **The national K-12 platforms are the real EMEA installed base, and this KB had never listed
  them** (`verticals/solutions.md`, added p132). **Librus** (Poland), **EduPage** (Slovakia and
  Czechia), **Pronote** (France), **IServ** (Germany) are the systems of record for grades,
  attendance and parent communication — and all four now have open-source MCP connectors, mostly
  MIT, all unofficial password-scrapers with **no sanctioned integration path, no LTI and no vendor
  agent programme**. 🟢 **This is the clearest unmet EMEA need in the KB:** a properly
  authenticated, logged, DPA-backed connector for a platform whose vendor has no agent story, in a
  jurisdiction where the data is children's. 🔴 **And the clearest hazard** — do not deploy the
  existing ones; `librus-mcp` only works where 2FA is disabled.
- 🆕 **EMEA is also the best-placed region in the new agent supply**, with nine of the twenty-one
  rows (Poland, Slovakia ×2, France, Germany, Finland, Bosnia, Norway, Russia) — the first time any
  region in this KB has had a double-digit placed count.
- **Platform reality:** EMEA procurement runs on ILIAS, OpenOLAT, Chamilo and Moodle far more than
  on Canvas, and all four have essentially no AI-on-top. 🟢 **Moodle is now better served than p130
  recorded:** five **MIT** standalone MCP servers exist (`repos`/`agents` pages), so a Moodle
  engagement no longer starts from a licence problem. Standards and accessibility compliance are
  scored in tenders. Richie (MIT, France) is proven at national scale for the public-facing layer.

- 🔴 **p133's EMEA demand probe returned nothing education-specific, and the reason is the query —
  recorded here rather than left looking like coverage.** The prescribed battery term *"AI education
  EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players"* returns enterprise IT: CompTIA's EMEA outlook, a Workday
  study from 2023, Bandwidth/Cavell telecoms research. **Zero results covered education ministries,
  university policy or EdTech vendors.** 🔵 **"EMEA" is a vendor sales-region word — no ministry,
  regulator or university describes itself that way**, so the term reaches people who sell *into*
  regions, not people who run schools. 🟢 **This is `P133-E`/`P132-A` on the demand side: the probe's
  vocabulary, not the region, produced the emptiness.** Later passes should query **country and
  institution names** (Skills England, Eurydice, the Council of the EU, individual Bundesländer) and
  aggregate to EMEA afterwards. 🟡 **Nothing here contradicts the EMEA findings above — those came
  from targeted country-level queries, which is exactly the point.**


#### 🟢 `ACTION BK` DISCHARGED AND REFUTING — EMEA demand was mis-queried, not saturated

🔴 **p133 recorded that the EMEA probe *"returned nothing education-specific"* and opened `ACTION BK`
to re-probe by country and institution rather than by the word "EMEA".** 🟢 **Run. It returns named
instruments with dates, and `ACTION BK`'s refutation condition — *"refuted if country-level queries
also return nothing new"* — is not met.** The region was never saturated; **"EMEA" is a vendor
sales-region word no ministry uses**, which is `P133-E` on the demand side.

| country / body | instrument | what it says |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **France** | **"L'IA en éducation : cadre d'usage"**, Ministry of Education, **June 2025** | 🟢 **A named national framework, and the strongest single EMEA artefact this KB has.** Requires **transparency and human oversight for AI-assisted decisions**; a fuller plan was expected **spring 2026**. 🔴 **No national monitoring body or dashboard has been set up** — so the oversight obligation exists with no state apparatus to discharge it, which is exactly where an assurance engagement sits. Also: the **Pix** platform carries a generative-AI literacy module (OECD DEO 2026) |
| 🟢 **European Commission** | **Ethical guidelines on the use of AI and data in teaching and learning** — **updated 2026** (first issued 2022) | Prepared by the Working Group on the Ethical Use of AI and Data in Education via the **European Digital Education Hub**. 🟢 **A 2026 refresh of the EU-level guidance is new information for this KB**, which had been treating the 2022 version as current |
| **Netherlands** | EU AI Act + **education inspectorate** guidance on digital learning tools | Schools operate under both. 🔵 **AI for Education conference, VU Amsterdam, 29–30 October 2026** — a dated, locatable convening |
| **Germany** | **North Rhine-Westphalia Ministry of School and Education**, via UNESCO **FutureProof** | Supports schools in adopting or updating AI strategies and school-level guidance. 🔴 **State-level, not federal** — and no federal or other-Land primary document was found |
| **Sweden** | **Skolverket** (National Agency for Education), a FutureProof partner | 🔴 **The only Nordic item found. No 2026 Nordic ministry guidance surfaced** |
| 🟢 **UK** | **Jisc** — year-long **AI marking and feedback pilots across 38 colleges and universities** | 🟢 **A named, institution-counted sector programme, and the most directly engagement-relevant EMEA item on this page**: a 38-institution consortium already running assessment-AI trials. Paired with student AI use in UK universities rising **66% → 92% in one year** |
| **UNESCO** | **Digital Learning Week**, Paris, **8–11 September 2026** | Expected to produce a **joint ministerial statement** on AI in schools |
| **OECD** | **Digital Education Outlook 2026** | 🔴 **~3 in 4 teachers say they lack the skills to teach with AI**, and **1 in 10 teachers report their school bans AI** in teaching |

🟢 **Supply, newly placed in EMEA by p134:** 🟢 **UNIwise ApS (Denmark)** publishing its own MIT
WISEflow integration — **a vendor counterparty, which this KB has been asking for since `P8`**;
**Open Assessment Technologies (Luxembourg)**, GPL-2.0; **France Université Numérique** with a second
MIT row, [`openfun/warren`](https://github.com/openfun/warren), **pushed on the day of this pass**;
**Zanichelli Editore (Italy)**; **3iPunt (Spain)**; **Universität Osnabrück (Germany)**.

🔵 **What `ACTION BK` actually taught this KB, beyond the rows.** 🔴 **A regional query is not a
regional measurement when the region is a sales abstraction.** "EMEA" is a seller's unit; ministries
legislate per country and consortia organise per sector. **Every future demand-side regional claim in
this KB must be built from country- or body-level probes and aggregated upward, never queried
top-down.** The same objection applies to "APAC" and "LATAM" and has not yet been tested there —
see `ACTION BM`.

🔴 **Instrument honesty on this table:** the France details came through a **third-party index blog**
and are **secondhand**; the Netherlands reading came from a school-focused blog. 🟢 The OECD, UNESCO,
EC and Jisc items trace to institutional sources. **No primary ministry document was fetched for
Germany, the Netherlands or any Nordic country** — recorded as an open gap, not as coverage.

#### 🆕 p135 — a European credential standard this KB had never recorded, and the probe failure confirmed

🔴 **`ACTION BM` re-confirms `P134-H` for EMEA on an independent run.** The bare probe
*"AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players"* returned, again, **enterprise IT**: CompTIA's
regional IT-strategy outlook, a **2023** Workday study on enterprise AI trust, and a February 2026
Bandwidth/Cavell piece on enterprise compliance complexity. The only education-sector source it
surfaced was the **Council of Europe's 2nd Working Conference on regulating AI in education** —
whose sitting was **24–25 October 2024**, with no indication whether its proposed European edtech
evaluation framework was adopted. 🟢 **Two independent passes, same failure: "EMEA" is not a word
the education sector publishes under.** Country-level probing remains mandatory here (p134's
`ACTION BK` discharge holds: France's *cadre d'usage*, NRW, Skolverket, Jisc's 38 institutions).

🟢 **Supply finding: ELM — the European Learner Model — is a live credential standard with real
code, and this KB has never listed it.** It appears in **three** repositories, always paired with
Open Badges 3.0 and W3C Verifiable Credentials:

| repo | licence | ★ | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`impierce/credential-converter`](https://github.com/impierce/credential-converter) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** | 12 | Translates between **Verifiable Credentials, Open Badges and ELM**, with a mapping CLI. Rust |
| [`impierce/digital-credential-data-models`](https://github.com/impierce/digital-credential-data-models) | not measured | 5 | The data models for OB 3.0 and ELM. Rust |
| [`educredentials/ec-issuer`](https://github.com/educredentials/ec-issuer) | 🔴 **no licence file** | 0 | Issues OB 3.0 and ELM credentials over **OIDC4VCI** |

🔵 **Why this is an EMEA fact and not a global one.** ELM is the EU's learner-credential data model;
it is the format a European qualification or micro-credential is expressed in for cross-border
recognition. 🟢 **For an EMEA engagement this is the mapping layer that makes a credential portable
between member states** — and the only permissive implementation is **Apache-2.0 and in Rust**,
which is a language mismatch for most AI work and therefore a concrete integration cost to quote.

🟢 **Two further EMEA institutional rows, both permissive, both pushed days before this pass:**
**Newcastle University's** [`numbas/Numbas`](https://github.com/numbas/Numbas) (Apache-2.0, 215★,
e-assessment, strongest in mathematics) and **frentix GmbH / University of Zurich's**
[`OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) (Apache-2.0 + attribution, 447★, a full LMS with
QTI and SCORM built in). 🔴 **Plus a copyleft one worth knowing by name:**
[`Citolab/qti-convert`](https://github.com/Citolab/qti-convert) (GPL-3.0), whose holder — read out
of its own filled-in GPL appendix — is **Stichting Cito Instituut voor Toetsontwikkeling, Arnhem**,
the Dutch national testing institute. 🟡 And **SURF**, the Dutch national education network, has
**archived** its `edubadges` front-end.

🔵 **The pattern across all five: EMEA's open-source education supply is institutionally owned —
universities, national testing institutes, national networks — and it is being published and then
sometimes abandoned.** An engagement here should expect to meet a credible institutional codebase
with no maintenance plan, which is a *partnership* conversation rather than a *procurement* one.

#### 🆕 p136 — the OECD's own report names what each ministry is building, and two gaps stay open

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** p135 recorded that the bare *"AI education EMEA"* probe fails
independently twice. 🟢 **It failed a third time this pass, and the country/institution probe again
returned dated, attributable material** — the `T10` rule holding for the fourth consecutive pass.

| country | what the OECD Digital Education Outlook 2026 and ministry sources say |
|---|---|
| **France** | 🟢 **Building a *sovereign* AI for education** — teacher lesson planning plus a chatbot — with AI literacy delivered through the state **Pix** platform (modules on prompting, how the systems work, and data). Governing instrument remains the **2025 *cadre d'usage***. 🟡 A secondary blog states it permits autonomous classroom use from **class 4e** upward; **not confirmed against the ministry text** and recorded at that strength |
| **Germany** | **KMK** (the standing conference of education ministers) issued national guidelines in **2024**; 🔴 **implementation belongs to the Länder**, who are writing their own plans from them. For a vendor this means **sixteen procurement conversations, not one** |
| **Netherlands** | 🟢 **NOLAI** — the national AI-in-education lab — is **building** educational GenAI tools as a government/academia/industry partnership. The one EMEA actor in this table that is a *builder* rather than a regulator |
| **Finland** | GenAI tested **primarily for teacher support and feedback** — the `T9` administrative-automation shape, not tutoring |

🔵 **Supply placed in EMEA this pass:** 🟢 **[`giantswarm/mcp-oauth`](https://github.com/giantswarm/mcp-oauth)
(Apache-2.0, German)** — the identity broker that refutes `T13`'s absence claim — and 🟢
**[`tkawen/developer-platform`](https://github.com/tkawen/developer-platform) (MIT, Algeria)**, the
**first North-African row** on this KB's supply pages. Plus three licence cells resolved to EMEA
institutional holders: **Stichting Cito** (`Citolab/qti-convert`, GPL-3.0),
**CoopCodeCommun** (`pyopenbadges`, LGPL-2.1) and — via lineage — **openHPI/KI-Campus**, whose two
slugs are now known to be one repository.

🔴 **Two gaps this pass could not close and will not paper over.** **Procurement returned nothing**
— no framework agreements, no tender data, no contract terms for AI in European education — and
**no Nordic university-level policy** was found, in a region whose *school-level* actors
(Skolverket, UNIwise, Cito) this KB already lists. Both are coverage holes.

### APAC

#### 🆕 🟢 p153 — TWO statutory high-impact regimes, and one of them is extraterritorial

🟢 **APAC is the only region where an education deployment meets a STATUTORY high-impact
regime TODAY, and this pass confirms it is two jurisdictions, not one.**

- 🟢 **Vietnam** — law **in force `2026-03-01`**, **extraterritorial**, with a government
  decision naming education uses (automated assessment, behavioural monitoring) as
  high-risk. 🟢 **Independently reproduced this pass on a second channel**, confirming
  `P149-B`.
- 🟢 **Korea** — AI Framework Act in force **`2026-01-22`**, education named a high-impact
  sector. 🔴 The decree setting the criteria remains unreadable (`P149-D`).
- 🔵 **So an extraterritorial obligation plus a named high-impact sector means a LATAM or
  EMEA vendor selling into Vietnam is in scope.** 🟢 **That is the sharpest single
  compliance fact on this page**, and it sells a conformity assessment to a client who does
  not operate in Asia.
- 🟡 **China** carries algorithm filings, synthetic-content labelling and security review
  for public models — 🟢 **a content-provenance engagement**, not a tutoring one.
- 🟡 **Japan** light-touch/voluntary · **Singapore** framework-and-toolkit (`AI Verify`) ·
  🔴 **India and Australia still have no national framework** · 🔴 **ASEAN's regional guide
  is voluntary.**
- 🔵 **A demand signal with a direction**: the Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 finds **lower**
  support for banning AI in schools across the Asian markets surveyed, and 🔴 **higher**
  support for banning in **Australia and New Zealand.** 🟢 **One region, two opposite
  buyer moods — so pitch enablement in Asia and safeguards in ANZ.**

🟡 **$2 282.9M (2025) at a 28.1% CAGR to 2033** is the regional figure found; 🔴 it is
market-research-class and is not adopted.


#### 🆕 🟢 p152 — Korea names EDUCATION, and a declared gap closes

🔴 **p151: *"APAC has no ministry-level education instrument beyond the five already
named."*** 🟢 **It has one now, and it is a statute in force.**

| | |
|---|---|
| **instrument** | **Korea — the AI Framework Act** (AI Basic Act), with its Enforcement Decree |
| **in force** | 🟢 **`2026-01-22`** |
| 🟢 **education is NAMED** | the *"high-impact AI"* definition covers sectors *"including energy, healthcare, nuclear operations, biometric data analysis, public decision-making, **education**, or other areas prescribed by Presidential Decree"* |
| **obligations on high-impact operators** | disclosure duties · human-oversight requirements · risk management |
| 🟡 **grace period** | one year before financial penalties — 🟡 **one Seoul-based secondary source**, unverified |
| 🔴 **the scope caveat, carried not buried** | 🔴 **One source reports that only Level-4 autonomous vehicles currently meet the high-impact threshold** — so the statutory list may be wider than the operative set, and an education system may not yet be caught |

🔴 **The education CRITERIA are not readable.** They sit in the Presidential Decree, and
🔴 **no primary Korean text could be fetched** — `P149-D` recorded this as an environment
limit and it is unchanged. 🟢 **So the useful statement is bounded**: education is named in
the statute; whether a given education system is high-impact depends on a decree this shelf
cannot read.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed.** 🟢 **Korea is now the APAC jurisdiction where an education-AI
engagement has a named compliance surface with a date behind it** — disclosure and human
oversight, not a conformity assessment. 🔵 **And it lands 23 months before the EU's
`2027-12-02`**, which makes Korea, not Europe, the first large market where an education
deployment meets a statutory high-impact regime. 🟢 **That reversal is worth saying in a
room.**

| **Japan** | 🟢 voluntary, light-touch. A working group has **proposed** a *Basic Act on the Advancement of Responsible AI* — 🔴 **"at a very early stage"** |
| **China** | 🔴 **the most assertive regulator in the region, and this pass found NO education-specific rule.** Registration and penalties are general |

#### 🆕 🟢 p149 — Vietnam's three education entries, NAMED (and p148's month figure withdrawn)

🟢 **`P149-B`: the listed education AI systems are three, and they are now specific** rather
than *"education is a listed sector"*:

| # | the listed education AI system |
|---|---|
| 1 | **automated content for learners' self-study** using 🔴 **uncontrolled data sources** |
| 2 | **automatically conducts examinations, assesses learning outcomes, or ranks learners** |
| 3 | **monitors and analyses learner behaviour** using 🔴 **biometric data** or other automated monitoring |

🔵 **Entry 2 is the whole of Korea's education scope; entry 3 is affect-adjacent inference,
which the EU prohibits rather than classifies.** 🟢 **So the three APAC/EU regimes differ in
kind, not in severity of the same rule** — Vietnam broadest in kind, EU strictest in
consequence, Korea narrowest in scope. 🔵 **An EU-shaped compliance package still transfers to
Vietnam more readily than to Korea**, because both are listed-sector regimes with conformity
assessment.

🔴 **Two corrections to p148's APAC headline.** 🔴 **"Twenty-one months earlier than Annex III"
is WITHDRAWN** — it is **nine** for 2027-03-01, and the earliest APAC date is **Korea's
~2027-01 at eleven months**, not Vietnam's (`P149-A`). 🟢 **And 2027-09-01 is the
education-specific deadline** — the later of Vietnam's two, for systems already running.

🔴 **The qualifier is still unsettled and the exposure is now sharper, not softer.** Allen &
Gledhill state *"not all AI systems used in these sectors will automatically be classified as
high-risk"* — 🟡 **so a narrowing exists and its text is unread.** 🔴 **Decision 33 itself is
unreachable from this environment** (`P149-E`), and 🔴 **Indochine Counsel notes the decision
says "finance" in an article and "banking" in the appendix without reconciling them** — a

🆕 🔴 **`T71`: Vietnam.** **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg** (issued **2026-06-30**, in force
**2026-08-15**) lists **46 high-risk AI systems** under **Law 134/2025** and **Decree 142**,
and 🔴 **education is a listed sector**, with automated assessment and behavioural
monitoring named as examples. Compliance **2027-03-01**; **2027-09-01** for education, health
and finance systems already in operation before 2026-08-15. 🔴 **Certain designated
high-risk systems need third-party certification before they may be operated**; others may
self-assess.

🔴 **So "Korea holds the only statutory education item in APAC" is withdrawn**, and the
region's sequence is now:

| date | jurisdiction | what comes due |
|---|---|---|
| **2026-01-22** | Korea | AI Basic Act in force; 🟡 **2026 is effectively a pilot year**, MSIT penalty grace ≥1 year |
| ~**2027-01** | Korea | penalties begin to bite on Art. 2(4)(j) student evaluation, **K-12 only** |
| 🆕 **2027-03-01** | 🆕 **Vietnam** | 🔴 **full compliance for listed high-risk AI, education included** |
| 🆕 **2027-09-01** | 🆕 **Vietnam** | 🔴 **legacy education/health/finance systems live before 2026-08-15** |
| **2027-12-02** | *(EU, for comparison)* | Annex III high-risk classification |

🟢 **Three APAC dates before the EU's.** 🔴 **"Twenty-one months earlier" is WITHDRAWN
(`P149-A`); the earliest is Korea's ~2027-01 at ELEVEN months.** 🔵 **Vietnam's framing is
the EU's, not Korea's** — a listed sector with
conformity assessment, rather than one named use case — so an EU-shaped compliance package
transfers to Vietnam more readily than to Korea.

🆕 🟡 **Taiwan passed an AI Basic Act in 2025-12.** 🔴 **Education scope unread** — recorded
as an item, not as an education claim.

🟢 **The rest of the region is unchanged and remains three poles at once**: **China**'s AI
compulsory **from age 6** (reported 2025-09) and the most comprehensive framework in the
region; **Korea**'s AI-digital-textbook **rollback** (textbook status stripped 2025-08-04;
adoption 37% → 19% of classes, ~4 190 → 2 095 schools); **ASEAN**'s Responsible AI Roadmap
(2025) phased through **2030**; Singapore, Japan and Australia governing by toolkit rather
than statute.

🔵 **Adoption breadth is extraordinary and depth is shallow** — one regional read, and it is
the condition a pilot-to-production practice is sold into. 🟡 **Named commercial players in
one market report**: Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's, with China, India and Japan
dominating; 🔴 **a commercial report, not independently verified.**

🟢 **The engagement shape:** two statutes with real dates, a region-wide skills gap, and no
education-specific adoption benchmark anybody could reach. 🔵 **Lead with the compliance
clock, which is now the nearest in the world.**

rather than a sector regime — the opposite of EMEA, where the education use case is named in the
classification itself.

🟢 **Korea's pilot year is the dated opportunity.** 🔵 **A statute in force with enforcement
deliberately deferred is the one window in which conformance work is cheap and voluntary**, and
it closes when the pilot does.

#### 🆕 p143 — fastest growth of the three majors, and the one region with no education-specific regulation named

🟡 **Market frame (publisher A, not adopted):** **$591.6M (2024) → $1 848.1M (2029)**, **20.9%

education-specific AI regulation for any APAC jurisdiction. What exists is general-purpose and
fragmented: Singapore with mature responsible-AI guidelines, China with laws against
algorithmic misconduct, the **ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and Ethics** still early-stage.

— so an APAC engagement is governed by general AI rules plus sectoral data-protection law, and
the compliance work is differently shaped rather than absent.

🔴 **And this page should not read that null as freedom.** 🔵 **Absence of an education-specific
instrument is absence of a published instrument**, and procurement conditions at national
education ministries are not indexed by the channels available here.

#### 🆕 p140 — APAC gets its first measured row AND turns out to own the Open edX deployment layer

🟢 **🆕 `ACTION CU` booked APAC on a compiled-in hostname.**
[`mcp-canvas-lms`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/mcp-canvas-lms) **1.0.5** hardcodes
`https://canvas.ust.hk/api/v1/courses` — the Canvas tenant of the **Hong Kong University of
Science and Technology**. 🔵 **The first APAC-placed connector row in this KB's history, and the
evidence is a request path rather than a name.**

🔴 **It is also a defect an institution would want flagged before installing it.** The package
reads `CANVAS_BASE_URL` in `dist/common/utils.js:4` and then ignores it in
`dist/operations/canvas.js:58`. 🔵 **One of its three tools queries HKUST's Canvas whatever the
client configures** — a data-egress question, invisible from the README, and the same class as
`eth-moodle-mcp`'s *"Works with Any Moodle"* heading, one step worse because this package claims
configurability and silently drops it.

🆕 🔵 **And a market fact this page should have carried for three years: the most widely used Open
edX deployment tool is owned in APAC.** [`overhangio/tutor`](https://github.com/overhangio/tutor)
declares `authors = [{name = "Edly"}, {email = "hello@edly.io"}]`. **Edly** is the edtech division
of **Arbisoft**, a **Pakistan**-based company, which acquired the French firm Overhang.IO on
**2023-03-27**; Tutor's founder **Régis Behmo** became Edly's VP of Engineering and still leads its
technical direction.

🔵 **This reframes a standing assumption.** This KB has treated the Open edX supply chain as
US-stewarded (Axim) with a European and North American contributor base. 🟢 **The deployment layer
every self-hosted Open edX installation passes through is owned by a Pakistani company** — which is
a live consideration for an APAC engagement and a sourcing option for any region. 🟡 **Recorded as
secondary evidence, not measured**: the acquisition is from company announcements and trade
reporting, and `edly.io` is a `.io` domain carrying no country signal, so the *artefact* evidence
is `hello@edly.io` and the regional attribution rests on the reporting.

🔴 **No new APAC instrument.** The channel returned Vietnam's Law `134/2025/QH15` (the statute
behind Decision 33, already carried), Korea's AI Basic Act of 2026-01-22, Taiwan's AI Basic Act and
Australia's National AI Plan — 🔵 **all already held.** 🟡 **The region's standing gaps hold**: the
compulsory-curriculum pairing (China + UAE) is unchanged, and no APAC education ministry's
provider map has been read.

#### 🆕 p139 — the compulsory-curriculum column has a second occupant, and the region owns the LMS defect

🟡 **🆕 China and the UAE are reported as the only two jurisdictions running a compulsory NATIONAL
AI curriculum, since the 2025-26 school year.** 🔵 **The pairing is the useful part**: this page has
tracked China's compulsory curriculum for three passes and recorded the Gulf as a region gap
(`P138-J`). 🔴 **If the UAE is genuinely the second, then the MEA gap is not an absence of
instruments but an absence of *reading* — which is exactly what `ACTION CO` failed to fix and why
the Gulf stays a gap rather than an absence.** Single secondary source; **not** quotable.

🟡 **🆕 India's legislative posture moved twice in 2026**: a **February 2026** amendment to the IT
Rules added obligations on AI-generated content, and in **July 2026** the government signalled it
may pursue **dedicated AI legislation** after relying on soft law. 🔵 **India is the largest
education market in the region and has been modelled here as "mandate without statute"; that
modelling now has an expiry date.**

🟢 **Confirmed, not new**: **South Korea's AI Basic Act in force 2026-01-22**, covering
"high-impact" AI **including education**, with human-oversight and disclosure duties (already on
this page at the APAC `#### p136/p137` entries); **India's AI and computational thinking mandatory
from Class 3 in 2026-27**, backed by **IndiaAI Mission** funding; **Japan's 2025 framework
promotion law** and its **AI Strategic Headquarters chaired by the Prime Minister**, light-touch
and voluntary; **Vietnam's Decision 33/2026/QĐ-TTg** as the only education-AI conformity regime
actually in force (`T16`).

🔴 **The region owns this pass's sharpest ownership defect, and it is the dominant open-source LMS
ERP pair.** `ACTION CJ` read source headers on rows p137 had cleared:

- 🔴 **[`frappe/lms`](https://github.com/frappe/lms)** (India) — its `license.txt` names **no holder
  of `frappe/lms`**; all three `Copyright` lines are the AGPL's own apparatus, including 🔴 **an
  unfilled `Copyright (C) <year>  <name of author>` template**. Ownership lives only in source
  headers, where it is **two separate legal entities**: `Frappe Technologies Pvt. Ltd.` (165 lines)
  and 🔴 **`FOSS United`** (78 lines).
- 🔴 **[`openeducat/openeducat_erp`](https://github.com/openeducat/openeducat_erp)** (India) — root
  `LICENSE` delegates to a **`COPYRIGHT` file that does not exist** anywhere in its 1 345 paths.

🔵 **Both are APAC-owned by their holders' incorporation, and both are rows a LATAM or EMEA client
would deploy** — so this is a supply-chain fact about the region's output, not a regional market
datum. 🔴 **Neither is booked as region *evidence*: the placement is inferred from an entity name,
which this KB does not accept as a region measurement.** Detail:
[`p139-source-header-holder`](../compose/code/p139-source-header-holder/README.md).


#### 🆕 p138 — `ACTION CG` in Vietnamese: the instrument gains an element, the transition date stays contradictory

🟢 **`ACTION CG` DISCHARGED — and it was the right language to ask in.** p137 recorded Vietnam's
Decision **33/2026/QĐ-TTg** from English-language law-firm briefings and published its transition
date as a conflict. Probed in Vietnamese, the instrument's own identifying facts firm up and **one
scope element p137 never recorded appears**:

| fact | measured this pass |
|---|---|
| signed | **2026-06-30**, by Deputy Prime Minister **Hồ Quốc Dũng** |
| in force | **2026-08-15** |
| issued under | 🆕 **Nghị định 142/2026/NĐ-CP**, the decree implementing the AI Law — p137 cited only Law **134/2025/QH15** |
| education entries in the high-risk list | self-study content generated from **uncontrolled data sources**; automated testing, **grading** and — 🆕 **`đánh giá tâm lý`, psychological assessment** — plus **monitoring and analysis of learner behaviour** |

🔴 **`đánh giá tâm lý` is the addition that changes a client conversation.** p137's reading was
content, grading and biometric monitoring. **Psychological assessment** is a separate class, and it
catches a category of product — wellbeing screening, engagement scoring, at-risk prediction — that
a vendor would not file under "grading" or "biometrics" and would therefore not expect to be
conformity-assessed.

🔴 **The transition date did NOT resolve, and it is published as a conflict for the second pass
running.** Two Vietnamese-language sources disagree:

- one gives **2027-09-01** for AI systems in health, **education** and finance, with **2027-03-01**
  for the remaining listed sectors;
- another gives **2027-03-01** for systems already operating before entry into force *and* for
  systems deployed within the first six months, and its visible scope list names ethnic and
  religious affairs, legal proceedings and transport — **not education**.

🔵 **So the disagreement is not about whether education has a later deadline; it is about whether
the later deadline exists at all.** Recorded as unresolved. For a 2027 engagement the safe planning
assumption is the earlier date, **2027-03-01**, because a client that is ready for March is ready
for September and not the reverse.

🔴 **The primary text was NOT read, and the reason is a hard channel failure, not an omission.**
`congbao.chinhphu.vn` — the Công báo gazette page carrying Decision 33 — **does not resolve in this
channel** (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`). p137 criticised itself for reading national instruments through
search results; this pass tried the gazette directly, failed at DNS, and says so. **Primary source,
secondary transport, third pass running.**

#### 🆕 p137 — APAC overtook the EU: Vietnam's education-AI conformity duties are in force NOW

🔴 **The first jurisdiction in which an education AI system carries conformity duties is not the EU.
It is Vietnam, and the date has already passed.**

| instrument | date | what it does |
|---|---|---|
| **AI Law No. 134/2025/QH15** | in force **2026-03-01** | risk-based national AI statute |
| **Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg** | issued **2026-06-30**, 🔴 **effective 2026-08-15** | lists **46 high-risk AI systems** across six sectors — 🔴 **three of them education** |

🔴 **The three education classes, as the sources render them:** systems that **generate self-study
content from uncontrolled data sources**; systems that **test, grade or rank learners**; systems that
**monitor learner behaviour using biometric data** (one summary extends this to other automated
monitoring). Duties attaching: **conformity assessment** (third-party or self-assessment, per the
decision's pathway), **registration in the National AI Database**, **human oversight** and
**incident reporting**.

🔴 **Transition dates conflict between two law-firm briefings** — one gives systems already running
**until 2027-09-01**, the other describes an unspecified additional period for systems live before
**2026-08-15**. Published as a conflict; `ACTION CG` probes the decree text in Vietnamese.

🟢 **China, read in Chinese (`ACTION BZ`), and it resolves a conflict this KB recorded as open.**
《"人工智能+教育"行动计划》, **教科信〔2026〕1号**, issued by the Ministry of Education **with five
ministries** and published **2026-04-10**, requires provinces to write AI curriculum guides fixing
objectives, content **and class hours per stage**. The hours come from
《中小学人工智能通识教育指南（2025年版）》: 🟢 **grades 1–2 ≥6 hours/year, grade 3 through junior
high ≥8 hours/year**, senior high as a required element of the IT subject. 🔵 **p136 recorded "6 or 8
hours a year depending on the source" as a source conflict. It is not a conflict — it is a per-stage
schedule, and the English sources dropped the stage.** Beijing's own 京教办〔2025〕2号 sets **≥8
hours/year for every school in the city from autumn 2025**.

🟢 **South Korea, read in Korean, and the rollback has a mechanism.** The MOE's **2026 업무계획**
(published 2025-12-12) puts **AI 보편교육** first of fifteen priority tasks:
**AI 중점학교 1 141 schools in 2026 → 2 000 by 2028**; AI-subject hours **doubled in primary
(34 → 68)** and **×1.5 in middle school (68 → 102)**; one device per student for grades 5–6 and
middle-2; **10G** school networks; **2 000 digital tutors**; a state-built **"K교육 AI"** for
teaching and administration. 🔴 **And p136's "stripped of official status" is precisely the August
2025 초중등교육법 amendment that demoted AI digital textbooks from 교과용 도서 (official textbook)
to 교육자료 (teaching material)** — school-by-school optional, not abolished. The national
**인공지능 기본계획 (2026–2028)** tasks the MOE with an information-strategy plan for an AI
teaching-and-learning platform by **Q3 2026**, and a stage-wise compulsory AI curriculum review with
the National Education Commission from **Q1 2027**.

🔵 **The commercial consequence, and it is new for this region:** APAC stops being "mandates without
a market". 🟢 **Vietnam is a compliance-engineering market with a date in the past**, and Korea is a
**procurement** market with countable units (1 141 schools, 2 000 tutors, 68 and 102 hours of
curriculum to fill).

- **Fastest-growing region (~35.3% CAGR), and the only one where curriculum mandates are driving
  demand from the top down.** China made AI a compulsory subject from age six as of September 2025.
  **India makes AI and computational thinking mandatory from Class 3 starting in the 2026-27
  academic year**, across government and private schools.
- **Regulation is real and heterogeneous — one compliance posture will not travel:** South Korea's
  **AI Basic Act took effect 22 January 2026**; China has six binding AI regulations including
  content-labelling and Cybersecurity Law amendments effective January 2026; India signalled in
  July 2026 that it may pursue dedicated legislation and already requires labels on AI-generated
  content; Japan's AI Promotion Act is light-touch, with a second AI Basic Plan approved by Cabinet
  on 14 July 2026; **Vietnam's Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg explicitly names education — including
  automated assessment and behavioural monitoring — as high-risk.**
- **Opportunity:** teacher-capacity tooling at national scale. A Class-3-onward AI mandate in India
  requires curriculum, teacher training and assessment at a volume no vendor can staff manually —
  this is a content-generation and teacher-support engagement, and `satvik314/educhain` (MIT) plus
  `GarethManning/education-agent-skills` are the closest existing bases. Supply-side advantage:
  APAC-origin open source is unusually strong here — `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0, 41k★),
  `frappe/lms`, `openeducat_erp`, and Moodle HQ itself.
- **Public attitudes are a real constraint:** support for *banning* AI in schools ranges from 23%
  (Indonesia) to 31% (South Korea), with India 26% and Japan 29% (Ipsos Education Monitor 2026).
  Roughly a quarter to a third of the public wants these tools out of classrooms.
- 🆕 **Australia and New Zealand entered this KB for the first time this pass (p132), and only as a
  regulatory signal:** Canberra is tightening AI governance and copyright rules, and **OpenAI has
  appointed a dedicated ANZ policy lead**. 🔴 **ANZ has been absent from every previous APAC
  section here** — which, given that Australia is a mature English-language higher-ed export market
  running Moodle and Canvas at scale, is a gap in this KB rather than a gap in the market. No
  ANZ-origin open-source education AI was found. Needs a dedicated probe next pass.
- 🆕 **APAC supply is now the deepest in the KB and it is curriculum-bound (p132).** Seven of the
  twenty-one new SIS/portal rows are APAC: India ×3 (`vtop-bhopal-mcp` Apache-2.0,
  `DAU-buddy` unlicensed, `AI-Based-Timetable-Generation-System` MIT), South Korea
  (`soongpt-plugin`, MIT, graduation-requirement reasoning), Taiwan (`nycu`, MIT), Vietnam
  (`qldt-hanu-mcp`, GPL-2.0) and China (`ddl-killer`). 🟢 **Two are built to a named national
  policy** — the timetable optimiser to India's **NEP 2020**, and `Tushar-cy/Offline-AI` to the
  Indian Class-10 curriculum — which is the pattern to expect where mandates drive demand from the
  top down, and the easiest kind of project to justify to a ministry.
- 🔴 **Jurisdiction warning on the Vietnam row:** `qldt-hanu-mcp` reads student records in the one
  APAC jurisdiction that **explicitly names education, automated assessment and behavioural
  monitoring as high-risk** (Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg). It is also the tier's only copyleft row.

- 🟢 **ANZ closes on both sides this pass, having been a stated blank (`ACTION BF`).** Demand:
  Canberra is tightening AI governance and copyright, and OpenAI has appointed a dedicated ANZ
  policy lead (Brent Thomas). Supply, new in p133: **Australia has the best-licensed national
  platform corner in this KB** — `BetterSEQTA/BetterSEQTA-Plus` (**MIT, 40★**, org-owned, 25 forks,
  a Chrome Web Store listing, pushed 2026-10-06) plus `BetterSEQTA/DesQTA` (**MIT, 16★**), both over
  **SEQTA**. 🔴 **New Zealand is one MIT row and it is archived**, killed by a **KAMAR API change**
  its README names explicitly — the clearest evidence in this KB for why pattern `P8` (sanctioned
  connector) beats a scraper.
- 🟡 **Governance lag, quantified regionally:** a Diligent Institute survey reports **57% of
  organisations in Asia** have put AI into at least one area of operations while governance
  frameworks struggle to keep pace. Pearson and TCS announced a multi-year AI learning alliance
  aimed at **employer** skills gaps rather than schools — consistent with this page's reading that
  APAC money moves through workforce programmes.


#### 🟢 p134 — two compulsory curricula, two regulatory extremes, and one number that reframes "adoption"

| country / body | finding |
|---|---|
| 🟢 **China** | **AI is a compulsory school subject from age six as of September 2025.** With the **UAE**, the only nations running a compulsory national AI curriculum since the 2025-26 school year. 🔴 **And the most binding AI regime in the region**: mandatory registration, content controls and data localisation — one review's conclusion is that it **requires a separate AI stack**, not a localised one. Labelling measures took effect **2025-09-01** |
| 🟢 **India** | **AI and computational thinking planned as mandatory from Class 3 in 2026-27.** IT intermediary-rule amendments took effect **2026-02-20** requiring **labelling of AI-generated content** on large platforms; in **July 2026** the government signalled it may pursue **dedicated AI legislation** |
| 🟢 **Singapore** | Strategy targets **AI leadership by 2030**, with **teacher training promised at every level by 2026**. Governance is voluntary and principles-based via the **AI Verify** testing toolkit. 🟢 **Highest measured diffusion in the region: 60.9% of working-age adults** |
| **Japan** | Voluntary, principles-based; **drafting legislation** while standing up **testing and assurance institutes**. 🔴 **Education-specific policy did not surface at all** — an informed gap, named rather than left blank |
| **Australia / NZ** | Carried forward from p133: Canberra tightening governance and copyright, against the best-licensed connector corner in this KB (`BetterSEQTA`, MIT, 40★). 🆕 p134 adds [`P1ckle3/blackboard-mcp`](https://github.com/P1ckle3/blackboard-mcp) — **University of Queensland Blackboard over Okta SSO**, 🔴 unlicensed |
| **region-wide** | 🔴 **Only 1 in 10 APAC enterprises considers itself "very mature" in AI adoption.** Ranking of readiness: **Singapore, then Australia, Korea, Taiwan**, with **Japan, India and China relative laggards** on enterprise adoption — 🔵 **note how badly that ranking fits the curriculum facts above**, where China and India are the most aggressive. **Enterprise maturity and education mandates are not the same axis, and a deck that conflates them will be wrong in both directions** |

🟢 **Supply, newly placed in APAC by p134:** [`lepo-project/roster-hub`](https://github.com/lepo-project/roster-hub)
(🔴 AGPL-3.0, Japan — OneRoster), the **e-Learning Consortium Japan**'s xAPI spec translation, and the
UQ Blackboard connector. 🔴 **No permissive, institutionally-owned APAC row in the standards tier** —
the region's two entries there are an AGPL-3.0 project and a translation.

🔵 **The engagement read:** China and India are **curriculum** markets (teacher capacity, content,
assessment at national scale — `compose/patterns.md` → **P5**), Singapore and Japan are **assurance**
markets (AI Verify conformance, testing institutes), and 🔴 **China is a market Globant should price
as a separate build**, not a localisation, on the registration and localisation requirements alone.

#### 🆕 p135 — `ACTION BM`: "APAC" fails exactly as "EMEA" does, and the country probe returns dated mandates

🔴 **The bare regional probe failed, and it failed in the same way.** *"AI education APAC 2026
adoption regulation players"* returned enterprise-technology coverage — Boomi's APAC tech
priorities, TechRepublic's enterprise signals, Forrester on sovereign-by-design execution,
Singapore's consultations on AI in **financial institutions** — and such education content as it
found was **vendor press release**: a Pearson/TCS learning alliance, LearnUpon's Sydney office,
Alteryx relaunching its academy. 🔴 **The run recorded explicitly that it found no education-specific
regulatory framework, no ministry curriculum rule and no edtech market-share data.**

🟢 **The country-level probe, run immediately after, returned the dated mandates the regional one
could not see:**

| country | obligation | date |
|---|---|---|
| **India** | 🔴 **AI and Computational Thinking become MANDATORY subjects from Class 3 upward**, aligned to the National Education Policy 2020, with teacher training through the **NISHTHA** programme | **from the 2026-27 academic year** |
| **Singapore** | An **AI Literacy Learning Framework** for primary and secondary students; enhanced mathematics, science and technology curricula; a **Guide to Using AI in Teaching**; 🟢 **digital education made a compulsory part of pre-service teacher training** | curricula **from 2026** |
| **South Korea** | Ministry of Education with the Ministry of Science and ICT treats AI as a **foundational literacy and instructional tool** rather than a standalone subject; the **2026 work plan** lists universal AI integration for students and teachers as a priority | **2026 work plan** |
| **China, UAE** | Mandatory AI literacy in national education, alongside India | in force |
| 🔴 **Japan** | 🔴 **MEASURED ABSENT.** The probe returned nothing on Japan's national AI-education policy. **This is a gap in this KB's coverage, not a finding that Japan has no policy** — and it is written down rather than left looking like coverage | — |

🟢 **Why this matters commercially, and it is the opposite of the EMEA shape.** India and Singapore
have made AI a **compulsory curriculum subject with a start date**, which creates demand for
*teacher capacity and curriculum materials at national scale* — not for governance programmes.
🔵 **A mandate with a date and a teacher-training programme attached (NISHTHA, Singapore's
pre-service requirement) is a delivery contract**, and it is procured by a ministry, not by an
institution. 🔴 Note the asymmetry that should temper any pitch: **OECD's 2026 review finds ~3 in 4
teachers lack the skills to teach with AI** (recorded by p134), against mandates that start in
months.

🔵 **Method, stated for reuse.** The failing probe and the succeeding one differ in one respect:
**the failing one names a vendor sales region, the succeeding one names a country whose ministry
publishes.** See the mechanism note under *LATAM* for why "LATAM" is the exception that proves it.

#### 🆕 p136 — `ACTION BT`: Japan is NOT a blank, it was a PROBE-LANGUAGE artefact, and Korea has reversed

**Pass 136, 2026-10-11.** p135 recorded *"🔴 **Japan: MEASURED ABSENT**"* and opened `ACTION BT` to
probe *"by ministry and in Japanese"*, predicting refutation *"if Japan has no national AI-education
policy instrument."* 🔴 **Japan has one, it is older than India's and Singapore's, and it was
issued as a ministerial circular.**

| instrument | detail |
|---|---|
| 「初等中等教育段階における生成AIの利活用に関するガイドライン（Ver.2.0）」 | **Published 2024-12-26** by MEXT, superseding the **Ver.1.0** interim guideline of **July 2023** |
| circular **6文科初第1895号** | **Dated 2024-12-26**, addressed to every **prefectural and designated-city board of education** and prefectural governor — 🟢 **an official instruction channel, not a blog post** |
| the operative principle | 🔵 AI output is *"参考の一つ"* — **one reference only**; the **human makes the final judgement and carries the responsibility**. Use must serve the 学習指導要領 competencies and **must not become an end in itself** |
| pupils' own use | 🔴 **Graduated by developmental stage**, with primary-school direct use requiring more caution; the **principal and teacher must confirm** the service's terms including age limits, parental understanding is required, and use is under teacher supervision |
| teachers' 校務 use | 🟢 **Actively encouraged** for efficiency and quality — explicitly framed as **働き方改革**, workload reform. This is `T9`'s administrative-automation driver, written into a national instrument |
| revision in flight | 🟢 **Opened 2026-06-30** at the Central Council for Education's digital-learning-infrastructure committee: **Ver.2.1 will not wait for the next 学習指導要領**, and the draft direction names **agentic AI**, **cognitive offloading (認知的オフロード)** and **assessment** |
| 生成AIパイロット校 | 🟡 Pilot-school programme continuing; one press report puts FY2026 at **~478 schools**. 🔴 **Not confirmed in an official document** and recorded as press-reported |

🟢 **The method finding generalises `T10` a third time, and it is the one to carry.** p135 narrowed
the failing-probe rule from *"regional acronyms fail"* to *"a region-scoped education body is what
predicts success."* 🔴 **Japan shows the body can exist and the probe still fail: MEXT publishes
prolifically, and an English-language probe found none of it.** The missing clause is the
**instrument's own language**. Queried as 文部科学省 / 生成AI / ガイドライン, the ministry's own PDFs
and circular numbers come back on the first attempt.

🔴 **And a reversal that corrects this page: South Korea's AI textbook mandate has been undone.**
p135 recorded Korea's MOE+MSIT 2026 work plan with AI as foundational literacy. This pass: adoption
sat **below 30% by March**, and in **August the National Assembly stripped the AI textbooks of
official status** after teachers' unions argued the pace had outrun preparation. The government's
answer is money rather than mandate — a **$960M** plan for AI talent centres and vocational high
schools **through 2030**. 🟢 **For a studio this is the most useful datapoint in the region**: the
first national AI-in-education mandate to be *rolled back*, and the reason given was **readiness,
not technology** — which is exactly the teacher-capacity engagement `compose/patterns.md` → **P5**
describes.

| country | the mandate, with this pass's numbers |
|---|---|
| **India** | **Classes 3–8 from the 2026-27 session; Classes 9–10 from 2027-28.** Teacher training via **NISHTHA**. 🟡 Reported backing: a **₹500 crore** centre of excellence inside the **₹10 372 crore** IndiaAI mission. 🟢 **18 000+ CBSE schools** already deliver a **15-hour SOAR** (Skilling for AI Readiness) module for Classes 6–8 |
| **China** | Compulsory **from age six**. 🔴 **Sources conflict on the dose: one says a minimum of 8 hours of AI instruction a year, another 6** — recorded unresolved. A 2026 national plan folds AI into **teacher qualification exams and certification**, targeting comprehensive AI-literacy infrastructure **by 2030** |
| **Singapore** | Narrower by design: AI modules inside **primary-level computer science**, **teacher AI training at every level by 2026**, Smart Nation targeting AI leadership **by 2030**, and **AICET** (hosted by AI Singapore) running projects **with the Ministry of Education** |
| **South Korea** | 🔴 **Reversed — see above** |
| **Japan** | 🟢 **Ver.2.0 since 2024-12-26; Ver.2.1 in progress — see above** |

🔴 **The informed gap: vendors and market share.** The APAC probe returned policy in depth and
**no edtech vendor names, no market sizing and no private-sector adoption figures** for India,
China or Singapore. The mandate side of this region is well covered here; the **demand-side
commercial map is not**.

### LATAM

#### 🆕 🟢 p153 — the governance gap on better numbers, and the depth qualifier that dissolves the tension

🟢 **The governance gap is intact and now rests on BETTER numbers than it did**, and the
figure pair that worried p152 has a settled publisher.

- 🟢 **The gap, from one study, three figures**: UNESCO **IESALC × UNU-IAS**, **200
  institutions / 19 countries** — **87%** use AI in at least one area, **74%** in teaching
  and learning, 🔴 **26%** have a formal AI strategy. 🟢 **A 61-point spread between doing
  and governing, inside one instrument's own population** — which is stronger than the
  cross-source ratio the shelf has been using.
- 🟢 **The `92%`/`79%` pair is DEC-origin** (`ACTION GJ`), 🔴 **with its sample still
  unread**, so it stays labelled and stays out of the load-bearing argument.
- 🆕 🔵 **And DEC's own second post dissolves the apparent tension**: **88% of LATAM
  faculty report "minimal" to "moderate" engagement.** 🟢 **High adoption, shallow usage —
  so a 79% engagement figure and a 26% strategy figure are describing the same
  institution**, and the sale is **depth**, not onboarding.
- 🟢 **The buyer has NO external deadline** (p152): on the EU timetable a LATAM institution
  faces nothing. 🟢 **So the sale is an ACCREDITATION and quality argument** — and the
  OECD's purpose-built-over-general-purpose line is the lever.
- 🟢 **Named counterparties that exist, with dates**: UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in
  Education for LAC** (launched **14 April**), its **Mexico pilot with CONALEP and DGETI**
  (**Aug 2026**), **Tec de Monterrey × UNESCO** (**Mar 2026**), and UNESCO AI-ethics
  training for officials in **Ecuador** and **Chile**.
- 🟡 **Policy, not law, is the regional state**: **Uruguay** first in LATAM to sign the
  Council of Europe **AI Framework Convention** (2025); **Colombia** via **CONPES 4144**
  (Feb 2025); **Mexico**'s federal AI bill still in the Senate; **Peru**'s framework
  pending implementation. 🔵 **No LATAM country yet imposes an education-specific
  statutory duty**, which is why the deadline is absent and the quality argument is all
  there is.


#### 🆕 🔴 p152 — a PROVENANCE TRAP on this page's own headline pair

🔴 **The `92%` students / `79%` faculty figures p151 published as *DEC × Tec de Monterrey*
are carried, in this pass's channel, by a GOOGLE FOR EDUCATION blog** — a vendor promoting
its own products into the market being measured.

🟡 **The pair is NOT withdrawn and NOT re-asserted.** 🔵 **Two readings are live and the
difference matters:**

1. 🟢 **p151's attribution is right** and the Google blog is re-reporting the DEC × Tec de
   Monterrey survey. 🔵 Then the figures stand and the blog is a third-hand carrier.
2. 🔴 **The pair ORIGINATES with the vendor blog** and p151 attributed a vendor's number to
   an academic survey. 🔴 **Then this page has a vendor figure in a governance argument.**

🔴 **This pass cannot tell which**, because the primary survey was not reached. 🔵 **`ACTION GJ`**
settles it by name. 🟢 **Recorded as a question about this shelf's own bookkeeping rather
than as a fact about the region** — `T82`'s class applied to a figure instead of a citation.

🔵 **What is NOT in question: the governance GAP ratio.** p151's two independent instruments
(DEC × Tec **30%**, UNESCO IESALC **26%** with governance) landed within 7% of each other on
the ratio of use to policy — 🟢 **and IESALC's 200 institutions across 19 countries is an
academic source unaffected by this trap.** 🟢 **So the gap argument survives even on the
worse reading of the pair.**

##### 🟢 Three new regional sources, each bounded

| source | figure | standing |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **McKinsey, 8-country LATAM study** (via *El Peruano*) | 🔴 **some institutions spending up to `8%` of revenue on AI** | 🟡 secondary; the expert quoted warns the challenge is not only financial |
| 🟢 **Digital Education Council** (via *Revista Economía*, II Encuentro Red Cordada, Peru) | **`71%` of students demand more training in applied AI ethics** | 🟡 secondary, conference-reported |
| 🟢 **Central America / Caribbean teaching study** | **280 teachers, 42 universities, 6 countries** — ChatGPT, Copilot, Gemini in teaching use | 🟢 **academic, and the first Central-America-specific population on this page** |

🔴 **Regulation: the absence is now a published academic finding, not this shelf's
inference.** A peer-reviewed article on AI regulation in Latin American higher education
states the region faces **a clear absence of specific regulatory frameworks**, naming
🔴 **Mexico** and 🔴 **Paraguay**. 🟢 **Chile still leads on policy; Brazil and Colombia have
strategies with no education rules; Mexico's are non-binding** — unchanged from p151.

🔴 **A trap avoided and named.** The same query returned Spain's `Real Decreto` prominently.
🔴 **Spain is EMEA.** 🟢 **It is filed under EMEA on this page and is NOT counted as a LATAM
instrument**, which a Spanish-language query makes easy to get wrong — 🔵 `P142`'s
Americas-disambiguation problem in the other direction.

🔵 **Opportunity, placed.** 🟢 **The buyer is identified and has already spent the money**:
institutions at up to 8% of revenue, ~**26–30%** with a published policy, and students
asking for ethics training at **71%**. 🔴 **The unserved line is governance and measurement,
not deployment** — and on the EU timetable, 🟢 **a LATAM institution now has no external
deadline at all**, which makes the sale a quality and accreditation argument rather than a
compliance one.

#### 🆕 🟢 p151 — a SECOND governance instrument, agreeing on the shape and differing on the level

🟢 **The Digital Education Council's *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026***, run with
**Tecnológico de Monterrey**'s Institute for the Future of Education; participating
institutions include **UNAM** (Mexico), **UPC** (Peru) and the **Pontificia Universidad
Católica de Chile**.

| measure | DEC 2026 | UNESCO IESALC (p150) |
|---|---|---|
| students actively engaging with AI | 🟢 **92%** | — |
| faculty actively engaging with AI | 🟢 **79%** | **74%** in teaching |
| institutions adopting AI in at least one area | — | 🟢 **87%** (n=200, 19 countries) |
| 🔴 **institutions with governance** | 🔴 **30%** — a *published policy* | 🔴 **26%** — a *formal framework* |
| the gap, as a ratio | 🔵 **3.1 : 1** | 🔵 **3.3 : 1** |

🔵 **Two instruments, two samples, two question wordings — and they are NOT averaged.**
🟢 **They agree on the shape**: adoption vastly exceeds governance. 🔴 **They differ on the
level by 4 points**, and *"has a published policy"* is a stricter test than *"has a formal
framework"*, so the direction of the difference is the wrong way round from what the
wordings predict. 🔵 **Recorded as a disagreement to carry, not a range to quote.** 🟢 **The
two ratios land within 7% of each other**, which is the more robust number of the four.

🆕 **And the regulatory map gains names this page did not have:**

| jurisdiction | state |
|---|---|
| **Chile** | 🟢 the most developed national AI policy in the region |
| **Brazil**, **Colombia** | 🟡 national AI strategies, 🔴 **no education-specific rules** |
| **Mexico** | 🟡 **SEP**, **ANUIES** and the Observatorio IA have issued recommendations — 🔴 **non-binding** |
| region-wide | 🔴 **no unified regulatory framework for AI in higher education** |

🆕 **A named index this page should be using**: the **IDB's ILIA** tracks readiness,
adoption and governance across **19 countries** — an instrument, not an aggregator, and the
first LATAM source on this page with a per-country structure.

🔴 **Both surveys are published by the institutions that ran them or their partners, and
both were read through aggregators this pass.** 🟢 Stated rather than implied.

🔵 **The opportunity is unchanged and now doubly measured: the governance gap IS the
engagement.** Two independent instruments put adoption at 3× governance. A LATAM higher-ed
engagement that ships a policy artefact — a published AI-use policy, an approval gate, an
access log — addresses the thing **70–74%** of institutions do not have.


#### 🆕 p150 — the governance gap is MEASURED: 87% adoption, 26% strategy, on a named sample

🟢 **This is the strongest regional evidence on this page, and it is LATAM's.** A **UNESCO
IESALC** study launched **September 2026** surveyed **200 higher-education institutions across
19 countries**:

| measure | value |
|---|---|
| 🟢 institutions using AI in **at least one area** | 🟢 **87%** |
| 🟢 using it in **teaching and learning** | 🟢 **74%** |
| 🔴 institutions with a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **26%** |

🔵 **A 3.3:1 adoption-to-governance ratio, on a sized and named sample.** 🟢 **This is the
figure to open a LATAM engagement with**, because it says the sale is not "adopt AI" — the
institution already has — it is **"govern what you already run."** 🔵 **`P10` and the
governance patterns are the LATAM-facing offer; a tutor pitch competes with something already
installed.**

🆕 🟢 **And the split by institution type tells a studio who buys:**

| institution type | AI implemented |
|---|---|
| private **non-profit** universities | 🟢 **84%** |
| **public** institutions | **68%** |
| private **for-profit** | 🔴 **52%** |

🔵 **The leader is the private non-profit tier, not the public system** — which inverts the
assumption behind a ministry-scale pitch and points the first conversation at university
consortia.

🟢 **K-12 teacher use, now with per-country numbers** (TALIS 2024, OECD mean **36%**):

| country | secondary teachers who used AI in the previous year |
|---|---|
| **Brazil** | **56%** |
| **Chile** | **55%** |
| **Colombia** | **53%** |
| **Costa Rica** | **52%** |
| 🆕 **Uruguay** (public schools, Ceibal) | 🟢 **75%** |

🟢 **Four LATAM countries 16–20pp above the OECD mean, as p148 recorded — and the named
figures now replace the range.** 🆕 **Uruguay's 75% via Ceibal is the highest single number on
this page for any region**, and it is a public-school measurement, not an institutional
self-report.

🆕 🟢 **A regional institution this page did not have**: UNESCO launched the **Observatory on
Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** on **2026-04-14**,
at **ECLAC headquarters in Santiago**. 🔵 **A named counterparty for a public-sector
engagement, and the first regional body whose remit is exactly this KB's subject.**

🟢 **Regulation — three distinct routes, none yet a statute in force:**

| country | instrument | state |
|---|---|---|
| **Brazil** | **PL 2.338/2023**, risk-based | 🟡 **passed the Senate Dec 2024, now in the Chamber of Deputies — the text can still change** |
| **Chile** | government-sponsored risk-based bill, introduced **2024-05-07** | 🔴 **still in first constitutional stage in committee** |
| **Colombia** | 🟢 **CONPES 4144** (Feb 2025) — policy, not statute | 🟢 **in force, government-wide AI programme with budget through 2030** |

🔵 **Colombia is the one to build against today**: a funded national programme with a budget
line through 2030 and no pending statute to invalidate the work. 🔴 **Brazil's text is still
movable, so a conformity dossier built to PL 2.338 now carries rework risk.**

#### 🆕 🟢 p148 — the first LATAM figure on this page that is a LEAD rather than a gap

🆕 🟢 **TALIS, secondary-teacher AI use in the previous year, against an OECD average of
36%:**

| country | teachers using AI | vs OECD mean |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Brazil** | **56%** | 🟢 **+20pp** |
| 🟢 **Chile** | **55%** | 🟢 **+19pp** |
| 🟢 **Colombia** | **53%** | 🟢 **+17pp** |
| 🟢 **Costa Rica** | **52%** | 🟢 **+16pp** |

🟢 **Four LATAM countries, all above the OECD mean, from one instrument with one
denominator.** 🔵 **Every LATAM figure this page has carried until now has been a
gap** — 87% adoption against 26% strategy (UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions / 19 countries),
53% of Brazilian school administrators against 22% of schools with guidelines, a
50%-want / 19%-practise feedback gap (DEC). 🟢 **This one is a capability.**

🆕 🟢 **Corroborated at national scale by a programme operator**: **Uruguay / Ceibal reports
75% of public-school teachers** using these tools.

🔵 **Why that changes the pitch.** The adoption-versus-governance gap is still real and still
the thing to sell into — 🔴 **87% / 26%** is the headline and it has not moved. 🟢 **But a
region whose teachers are 16–20 points ahead of the OECD on usage is not an adoption
problem**, it is a **governance and enablement** problem on a workforce that has already
adopted. 🔵 **Sell the frameworks, the data protection and the teacher-facing tooling; do not
sell adoption to people who are leading it.**

🆕 🟢 **A regional institution now exists to sell into and partner with**: the **UNESCO
Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean**,
launched **2026-04-14**, explicitly a platform to support states integrating AI into
education systems. 🟢 **UNESCO also runs AI ethics and regulation training for officials in
Ecuador and Chile.**

🟢 **Regulation, unchanged and worth restating precisely** — all general AI law, none
education-specific:

| country | instrument | stage |
|---|---|---|
| Brazil | **PL 2.338/2023** | 🟡 **in the Chamber of Deputies — text can still change** |
| Chile | government bill, risk-based | 🟡 **first constitutional stage**, Commission on Future, Science and Technology |
| Colombia | **CONPES 4144** | 🟢 **adopted 2025-02** — a national AI policy, not a statute |

🔵 **So LATAM has the region's strongest teacher adoption, no education-specific statute, and
a UNESCO-backed regional body.** 🟢 **The opening is governance-as-a-service on an installed
base**, and the Google.org / Raspberry Pi Foundation programme (**$4.6M**, **24 000**
educators, **1.25M students by 2028**, **nine countries**) is the template for how that work
gets funded here.

🟢 **And the instrument-side news is LATAM's too.** `ACTION ER` added **16 region placements**
to the connector tier this pass and 🟢 **almost all of them are LATAM rows** — the offset
channel had been declining `-0300` as ambiguous while the repositories' own code named
`sigaa.ufpi.br`, `guarani.unq.edu.ar`, `aulavirtual.upc.edu.pe`. 🔵 **This KB's LATAM
coverage was being suppressed by a timezone, not by a lack of evidence.**
